coefficient\nBASELINE_WIN = 300           # frames (~19.4 s) for the maximin filter (\"20 s sliding window\")\nBASELINE_SMOOTH_SIG = 15     # frames, Gaussian sigma along time before the maximin filter\nDFF_SMOOTH_SIG = 2           # frames (~0.129 s) Gaussian smoothing of dF/F\nOASIS_TAU = 0.7              # preprocessing.dff default (suite2p tau for fast GCaMP)\nOASIS_BATCH = 2000\n\n# Methods: \"putative interneurons ... Pearson correlation of >0.5 between their dF/F\n# timeseries and the animal's running speed\"\nINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR = 0.5\n\n# Methods: lick-sensor error trials -- \">30% of the 0.0645 s imaging frame samples in the\n# trial containing a cumulative lick count >2\"\nLICK_ERROR_FRAC_THR = 0.3\nLICK_ERROR_COUNT_THR = 2\n\n# Decoder output discretisation\nDIST_EDGES = (-50.0, -10.0, 0.0, 10.0, 50.0)\nPOS_EDGES = (90.0, 180.0, 270.0, 360.0)\nSPEED_EDGES = (2.0, 10.0, 20.0, 40.0)\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_trial_start', 'environment', 'trial_number',\n               'previous_trial_rewarded']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['reward_zone_distance', 'position', 'speed', 'lick',\n                'reward_zone_location', 'reward_outcome']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [\n    ['< -50 cm', '-50 to -10 cm', '-10 to <0 cm', '0 cm (in reward zone)',\n     '>0 to +10 cm', '+10 to +50 cm', '> +50 cm'],\n    ['< 90 cm', '90-180 cm', '180-270 cm', '270-360 cm', '> 360 cm'],\n    ['< 2 cm/s', '2-10 cm/s', '10-20 cm/s', '20-40 cm/s', '> 40 cm/s'],\n    ['no lick', 'lick'],\n    ['A (80-130 cm)', 'B (200-250 cm)', 'C (320-370 cm)'],\n    ['omitted', 'rewarded'],\n]\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Signal processing helpers (ports of the reference code)\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef nansmooth(a, sig, axis=None):\n    \"\"\"NaN-tolerant Gaussian smoothing (TwoPUtils.utilities.nansmooth / ut.nansmooth).\n\n    `sig` may be a scalar (smooth along `axis`) or a per-axis list (n-d smoothing).\n    \"\"\"\n    nan_inds = np.isnan(a)\n    a_nanless = np.where(nan_inds, 0.0, a)\n    one = np.ones(a.shape, dtype=a.dtype)\n    one[nan_inds] = 0.001\n    if np.isscalar(sig):\n        a_nanless = gaussian_filter1d(a_nanless, sig, axis=axis)\n        one = gaussian_filter1d(one, sig, axis=axis)\n    else:\n        a_nanless = gaussian_filter(a_nanless, sig)\n        one = gaussian_filter(one, sig)\n    return a_nanless / one\n\n\ndef dff_and_events(f, f_neu, trial_starts, teleports, frame_rate,\n                   neu_coef=NEU_COEF, tau=OASIS_TAU):\n    \"\"\"Port of `reward_relative.preprocessing.dff` for the single-channel,\n    `neuropil_method='subtract'`, `baseline_method='maximin'`, `deconvolve=True`,\n    `keep_teleports=False` configuration used for this dataset.\n\n    Args:\n        f:        (n_cells, n_frames) raw ROI fluorescence\n        f_neu:    (n_cells, n_frames) neuropil fluorescence\n        trial_starts, teleports: frame indices of trial starts / teleports\n        frame_rate: imaging rate per plane (Hz)\n\n    Returns:\n        dff:   (n_cells, n_frames) float32, NaN outside trials\n        spks:  (n_cells, n_frames) float32 deconvolved \"events\", NaN outside trials\n\n    suite2p's OASIS implementation -- the one the reference code calls -- is imported\n    lazily so the parent process never initialises its OpenMP runtime before forking.\n    \"\"\"\n    from suite2p.extraction import dcnv\n\n    f = f.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n    f_neu = f_neu.astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n\n    f_ = np.full(f.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    f_neu_ = np.full(f_neu.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n\n    # keep_teleports=False -> only on-track samples, window [start-1, stop-1)\n    slices = [slice(s - 1, t - 1) for s, t in zip(trial_starts, teleports)]\n    for sl in slices:\n        f_[:, sl] = f[:, sl]\n        f_neu_[:, sl] = f_neu[:, sl]\n\n    nanmask = ~np.isnan(f_[0, :])\n\n    # neuropil correction\n    f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n\n    flow = np.full(f_.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    spks = np.full(f_.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n\n    for sl in slices:\n        # add the per-trial neuropil mean back in so dF/F is not divided by a small number\n        f_[:, sl] = f_[:, sl] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(f_neu_[:, sl], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        # maximin baseline: smooth, 20 s minimum filter, then same-window maximum filter\n        base = nansmooth(f_[:, sl], [0, BASELINE_SMOOTH_SIG])\n        base = minimum_filter1d(base, BASELINE_WIN, axis=-1)\n        base = maximum_filter1d(base, BASELINE_WIN, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, sl] = base\n\n    dff = np.full(f_.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n\n    for sl in slices:\n        smoothed = nansmooth(dff[:, sl], DFF_SMOOTH_SIG, axis=1)\n        dff[:, sl] = smoothed\n        spks[:, sl] = dcnv.oasis(np.ascontiguousarray(smoothed, dtype=np.float32),\n                                 OASIS_BATCH, tau, frame_rate)\n\n    return dff, spks\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Task-variable helpers (ports of the reference code)\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef zone_labels_from_scene(scene, ntrials, change_trial=CHANGE_TRIAL):\n    \"\"\"Port of `reward_relative.behavior.get_reward_zones` label logic.\n\n    Scene names are either '<Env>_Location<Z>' (fixed zone) or a switch scene\n    containing '<Z1>_to' and ending in '<Z2>' (e.g. 'Env1_LocationB_to_A',\n    'Env1_C_to_Env2_B'), in which case the zone changes after `change_trial` trials.\n    \"\"\"\n    for z in ZONE_ORDER:\n        if scene.endswith('Location' + z):\n            return np.array([z] * ntrials)\n    first = None\n    for z in ZONE_ORDER:\n        if z + '_to' in scene:\n            first = z\n    last = scene[-1]\n    if first is None or last not in ZONE_ORDER:\n        raise ValueError(f'Unrecognised scene name: {scene}')\n    n0 = min(change_trial, ntrials)\n    return np.array([first] * n0 + [last] * (ntrials - n0))\n\n\ndef signed_distance_to_zone(pos, zone_start, zone_end):\n    \"\"\"Signed distance (cm) from `pos` to the nearest point of [zone_start, zone_end].\n\n    0 inside the zone, negative before it, positive after it.\n    \"\"\"\n    d = np.zeros_like(pos)\n    before = pos < zone_start\n    after = pos > zone_end\n    d[before] = pos[before] - zone_start\n    d[after] = pos[after] - zone_end\n    return d\n\n\ndef discretize_distance(d):\n    \"\"\"7 classes, exactly as specified by the decoder task.\"\"\"\n    out = np.full(d.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)     # d == 0 -> in the reward zone\n    out[d < 0] = 2                                # -10 <= d < 0\n    out[d < DIST_EDGES[1]] = 1                    # -50 <= d < -10\n    out[d < DIST_EDGES[0]] = 0                    # d < -50\n    out[d > 0] = 4                                # 0 < d <= 10\n    out[d > DIST_EDGES[3]] = 5                    # 10 < d <= 50\n    out[d > DIST_EDGES[4]] = 6                    # d > 50\n    return out\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Per-session conversion\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef read_session(path):\n    \"\"\"Read everything needed from one NWB file using pynwb.\n\n    Returns a dict of raw (unprocessed) arrays.\n    \"\"\"\n    with NWBHDF5IO(path, 'r', load_namespaces=True) as io:\n        nwb = io.read()\n        scene = nwb.identifier.split('/')[-1]\n        subject = nwb.subject.subject_id\n        session_id = nwb.session_id\n\n        b = nwb.processing['behavior'].data_interfaces['BehavioralTimeSeries'].time_series\n        ts = np.asarray(b['position'].timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n        beh = {k: np.asarray(b[k].data[:], dtype=np.float64) for k in\n               ['position', 'speed', 'lick', 'reward_zone', 'environment',\n                'trial number', 'trial_start', 'teleport', 'scanning']}\n        reward_times = np.asarray(b['Reward'].timestamps[:], dtype=np.float64)\n\n        oph = nwb.processing['ophys'].data_interfaces\n        seg = oph['ImageSegmentation'].plane_segmentations['PlaneSegmentation']\n        iscell = np.asarray(seg['iscell'].data[:])[:, 0].astype(bool)\n        plane_idx = np.asarray(seg['planeIdx'].data[:]).astype(int)\n\n        # Concatenate the per-plane RoiResponseSeries; their ROI ids index the shared\n        # PlaneSegmentation table in order, so plane0 then plane1 reproduces its row order.\n        plane_names = sorted(oph['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series.keys())\n        f_list, fneu_list, roi_ids = [], [], []\n        for pn in plane_names:\n            rrs = oph['Fluorescence'].roi_response_series[pn]\n            rid = np.asarray(rrs.rois.data[:], dtype=int)\n            sel = iscell[rid]                       # only load curated cells\n            roi_ids.append(rid[sel])\n            f_list.append(np.asarray(rrs.data[:, :], dtype=np.float32)[:, sel].T)\n            nrs = oph['Neuropil'].roi_response_series[pn]\n            fneu_list.append(np.asarray(nrs.data[:, :], dtype=np.float32)[:, sel].T)\n        f = np.concatenate(f_list, axis=0)\n        f_neu = np.concatenate(fneu_list, axis=0)\n        roi_ids = np.concatenate(roi_ids)\n        imaging_rate = float(nwb.imaging_planes['ImagingPlane'].imaging_rate)\n        location = nwb.imaging_planes['ImagingPlane'].location\n\n    # A handful of the 2-plane files (10 of 152) carry one more imaging frame than the\n    # 2P-aligned VR data. The VR alignment defines the common sample grid, so trim the\n    # trailing frames of every stream to the shorter length.\n    n = min(len(ts), f.shape[1])\n    n_trimmed = max(len(ts), f.shape[1]) - n\n    ts = ts[:n]\n    beh = {k: v[:n] for k, v in beh.items()}\n    f, f_neu = f[:, :n], f_neu[:, :n]\n\n    return dict(path=path, scene=scene, subject=subject, session_id=session_id,\n                ts=ts, reward_times=reward_times, f=f, f_neu=f_neu,\n                roi_ids=roi_ids, plane_of_cell=plane_idx[roi_ids], n_trimmed=n_trimmed,\n                imaging_rate=imaging_rate, location=location, **beh)\n\n\ndef convert_session(path, show_processing=False, plot_dir='/app', signal='dff'):\n    \"\"\"Convert one NWB session into per-trial neural / input / output arrays.\"\"\"\n    t0 = time.time()\n    raw = read_session(path)\n    t_read = time.time() - t0\n\n    ts = raw['ts']\n    pos = raw['position']\n    speed = raw['speed']\n    lick = raw['lick']\n    rzone_flag = raw['reward_zone']\n    morph = raw['environment']\n    scanning = raw['scanning']\n\n    si = np.where(raw['trial_start'] == 1)[0]\n    ti = np.where(raw['teleport'] == 1)[0]\n    assert len(si) == len(ti), f'{path}: {len(si)} starts vs {len(ti)} teleports'\n    # guard against a trial starting on frame 0 (window would wrap around)\n    keep = (si >= 1) & (ti <= len(ts))\n    n_trials_out_of_range = int((~keep).sum())\n    si, ti = si[keep], ti[keep]\n    assert np.all(ti > si), f'{path}: teleport before trial start'\n    ntrials_all = len(si)\n\n    # The frame period is the sampling interval of the (already 2P-aligned) VR data.\n    dt = float(np.median(np.diff(ts)))\n    frame_rate = 1.0 / dt\n\n    # ---- neural: dF/F + deconvolved events (reference preprocessing.dff) -------------\n    t1 = time.time()\n    dff, events = dff_and_events(raw['f'], raw['f_neu'], si, ti, frame_rate)\n    t_dff = time.time() - t1\n\n    valid = ~np.isnan(dff[0, :])          # frames inside a trial window\n\n    # ---- neuron curation: putative interneurons (r(dF/F, speed) > 0.5) ---------------\n    sp_v = speed[valid].astype(np.float32)\n    d_v = dff[:, valid]\n    sp_c = sp_v - sp_v.mean()\n    d_c = d_v - d_v.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n    denom = np.sqrt((d_c ** 2).sum(axis=1) * (sp_c ** 2).sum())\n    with np.errstate(invalid='ignore', divide='ignore'):\n        r_speed = (d_c @ sp_c) / denom\n    r_speed = np.nan_to_num(r_speed, nan=0.0)\n    keep_cells = r_speed <= INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR\n    n_interneuron = int((~keep_cells).sum())\n    dff = dff[keep_cells]\n    events = events[keep_cells]\n    plane_of_cell = raw['plane_of_cell'][keep_cells]\n\n    # ---- per-trial task variables (reference behavior.get_trial_types/_reward_zones) --\n    reward_idx = np.searchsorted(ts, raw['reward_times'])\n    zone_lab = zone_labels_from_scene(raw['scene'], ntrials_all)\n    zone_start = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_DICT[z][0] for z in zone_lab])\n    zone_end = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_DICT[z][1] for z in zone_lab])\n    zone_code = np.array([ZONE_ORDER.index(z) for z in zone_lab], dtype=np.int64)\n\n    isreward = np.zeros(ntrials_all, dtype=np.int64)\n    trial_morph = np.zeros(ntrials_all, dtype=np.int64)\n    lick_error = np.zeros(ntrials_all, dtype=bool)\n    scan_bad = np.zeros(ntrials_all, dtype=bool)\n    for i, (s, e) in enumerate(zip(si, ti)):\n        has_reward = np.any((reward_idx >= s) & (reward_idx < e))\n        isreward[i] = int(has_reward and np.any(rzone_flag[s:e] > 0))\n        mvals = np.unique(morph[s:e])\n        trial_morph[i] = int(mvals[0]) if len(mvals) == 1 else int(np.round(np.median(morph[s:e])))\n        L = lick[s:e]\n        lick_error[i] = (np.sum(L > LICK_ERROR_COUNT_THR) / len(L)) > LICK_ERROR_FRAC_THR\n        scan_bad[i] = np.any(scanning[s - 1:e - 1] != 1)\n\n    # previous-trial outcome; index 0 is undefined -> 0 (see CONVERSION_NOTES Step 5)\n    prev_reward = np.zeros(ntrials_all, dtype=np.int64)\n    prev_reward[1:] = isreward[:-1]\n\n    # ---- assemble per-trial arrays ---------------------------------------------------\n    neural_trials, input_trials, output_trials = [], [], []\n    kept_trial_idx = []\n    n_dropped_lick, n_dropped_scan, n_dropped_nan = 0, 0, 0\n    for i, (s, e) in enumerate(zip(si, ti)):\n        if lick_error[i]:\n            n_dropped_lick += 1\n            continue\n        if scan_bad[i]:\n            n_dropped_scan += 1\n            continue\n        sl = slice(s - 1, e - 1)\n        act = (events if signal == 'events' else dff)[:, sl]\n        if act.shape[1] < 2 or not np.all(np.isfinite(act)):\n            n_dropped_nan += 1\n            continue\n\n        T = act.shape[1]\n        p = pos[sl]\n        sp_t = speed[sl]\n        lk = lick[sl]\n\n        inp = np.empty((4, T), dtype=np.float32)\n        inp[0] = ts[sl] - ts[s]                 # t = 0 at the trial_start frame\n        inp[1] = trial_morph[i]\n        inp[2] = i\n        inp[3] = prev_reward[i]\n\n        out = np.empty((6, T), dtype=np.int64)\n        out[0] = discretize_distance(signed_distance_to_zone(p, zone_start[i], zone_end[i]))\n        out[1] = np.digitize(p, POS_EDGES)\n        out[2] = np.digitize(sp_t, SPEED_EDGES)\n        out[3] = (lk > 0).astype(np.int64)\n        out[4] = zone_code[i]\n        out[5] = isreward[i]\n\n        neural_trials.append(np.ascontiguousarray(act, dtype=np.float32))\n        input_trials.append(inp)\n        output_trials.append(out)\n        kept_trial_idx.append(i)\n\n    info = dict(\n        path=path, subject=raw['subject'], session_id=raw['session_id'],\n        scene=raw['scene'], location=raw['location'],\n        n_trials_all=ntrials_all, n_trials_kept=len(neural_trials),\n        n_dropped_lick=n_dropped_lick, n_dropped_scan=n_dropped_scan,\n        n_dropped_nan=n_dropped_nan,\n        n_cells_iscell=int(raw['f'].shape[0]), n_interneuron=n_interneuron,\n        n_frames_trimmed=int(raw['n_trimmed']),\n        n_trials_out_of_range=n_trials_out_of_range,\n        n_cells=int(dff.shape[0]), dt=dt, imaging_rate=raw['imaging_rate'],\n        n_omission=int((isreward == 0).sum()),\n        zone_labels=''.join(zone_lab), morph=trial_morph.tolist(),\n        t_read=t_read, t_dff=t_dff, t_total=time.time() - t0,\n        dff_median=float(np.nanmedian(dff[:, valid])),\n        dff_p99=float(np.nanpercentile(dff[:, valid], 99)),\n        events_frac_zero=float(np.mean(events[:, valid] == 0)),\n    )\n\n    if show_processing:\n        plot_processing(raw, dff, events, si, ti, valid, zone_start, zone_end,\n                        zone_lab, isreward, kept_trial_idx, neural_trials,\n                        input_trials, output_trials, r_speed, keep_cells,\n                        plot_dir, signal)\n\n    brain_region_idx = np.zeros(dff.shape[0], dtype=np.int64)\n    return dict(neural=neural_trials, input=input_trials, output=output_trials,\n                brain_region_idx=brain_region_idx, info=info,\n                plane_of_cell=plane_of_cell)\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Diagnostic plots\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef plot_processing(raw, dff, events, si, ti, valid, zone_start, zone_end, zone_lab,\n                    isreward, kept_trial_idx, neural_trials, input_trials,\n                    output_trials, r_speed, keep_cells, plot_dir, signal='dff'):\n    import matplotlib\n    matplotlib.use('Agg')\n    import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n    tag = f\"{raw['subject']}_ses-{raw['session_id']}\"\n    ts, pos, speed, lick = raw['ts'], raw['position'], raw['speed'], raw['lick']\n\n    fig, axes = plt.subplots(9, 1, figsize=(20, 26))\n\n    # --- 1. raw F / neuropil / baseline-corrected dF/F for one example cell ----------\n    cell = int(np.nanargmax(np.nanstd(dff, axis=1)))\n    w = slice(si[0] - 1, ti[4] - 1)          # first five trials\n    ax = axes[0]\n    ax.plot(ts[w], raw['f'][cell, w], lw=0.7, label='raw F')\n    ax.plot(ts[w], raw['f_neu'][cell, w], lw=0.7, label='neuropil')\n    for s, e in zip(si[:5], ti[:5]):\n        ax.axvspan(ts[s], ts[e - 1], color='0.9', zorder=0)\n    ax.set_title(f'{tag}: raw fluorescence, example cell {cell} (grey = trials)')\n    ax.legend(loc='upper right')\n    ax.set_ylabel('a.u.')\n\n    ax = axes[1]\n    ax.plot(ts[w], dff[cell, w], lw=0.8, label='dF/F (smoothed)')\n    ax.plot(ts[w], events[cell, w], lw=0.8, label='events (OASIS)')\n    for s, e in zip(si[:5], ti[:5]):\n        ax.axvspan(ts[s], ts[e - 1], color='0.9', zorder=0)\n    ax.set_title('dF/F and deconvolved events (NaN between trials = gaps)')\n    ax.legend(loc='upper right')\n\n    # --- 2. dF/F distribution and interneuron filter ---------------------------------\n    ax = axes[2]\n    ax.hist(dff[:, valid].ravel()[::101], bins=200, log=True)\n    ax.set_title(f'dF/F ax.axvline(INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THR, color='r')\n    ax.set_title(f'Pearson r(dF/F, speed) per cell; {int((~keep_cells).sum())} of '\n                 f'{len(keep_cells)} excluded as putative   ax.fill_between([ts[s], ts[e - 1]], zone_start[j], zone_end[j],\n                        color='orange', alpha=.3)\n    ax.set_ylabel('position (cm)')\n    ax.set_title('position, trial starts (green) / ends (red), reward zone (orange)')\n\n    ax = axes[5]\n    ax.plot(ts[w2], speed[w2], lw=1, label='speed (cm/s)')\n    ax.plot(ts[w2], lick[w2] * 5, lw=1, label='lick count x5')\n    rt = raw['reward_times']\n    rt = rt[(rt >= ts[w2.start]) & (rt <= ts[w2.stop])]\n    ax.plot(rt, np.zeros_like(rt) + 50, 'v', color='m', label='reward')\n    ax.legend(loc='upper right')\n    ax.set_title('speed / licks / reward delivery')\n\n    # --- 4. discretisation check: continuous vs class, one trial ---------------------\n    tr = min(5, len(neural_trials) - 1)\n    orig = kept_trial_idx[tr]\n    sl = slice(si[orig] - 1, ti[orig] - 1)\n    tt = input_trials[tr][0]\n    d = signed_distance_to_zone(pos[sl], zone_start[orig], zone_end[orig])\n    ax = axes[6]\n    ax.plot(tt, pos[sl], 'k', label='position (cm)')\n    ax.plot(tt, d, 'b', label='distance to reward zone (cm)')\n    for y in (-50, -10, 0, 10, 50):\n        ax.axhline(y, color='b', ls=':', lw=.5)\n    ax2 = ax.twinx()\n    ax2.step(tt, output_trials[tr][0], 'r', where='post', label='distance class')\n    ax2.step(tt, output_trials[tr][1], 'g', where='post', label='position class')\n    ax2.set_ylabel('class')\n    ax.legend(loc='upper left')\n    ax2.legend(loc='lower right')\n    ax.set_title(f'trial {orig}: discretisation of distance-to-zone and position '\n                 f'(zone {zone_lab[orig]}, rewarded={isreward[orig]})')\n    ax.set_xlabel('time from trial start (s)')\n\n    ax = axes[7]\n    ax.plot(tt, speed[sl], 'k', label='speed (cm/s)')\n    for y in (2, 10, 20, 40):\n        ax.axhline(y, color='b', ls=':', lw=.5)\n    ax2 = ax.twinx()\n    ax2.step(tt, output_trials[tr][2], 'r', where='post', label='speed class')\n    ax2.step(tt, output_trials[tr][3], of speed and licking')\n    ax.set_xlabel('time from trial start (s)')\n\n    # --- 5. final aligned trial: neural raster + outputs ------------------------------\n    ax = axes[8]\n    act = flush=True)\n\n\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Driver\n# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ndef _worker(args):\n    path, show, plot_dir, signal = args\n    warnings.filterwarnings('ignore')\n    try:\n        return convert_session(path, show_processing=show, plot_dir=plot_dir,\n                               signal=signal)\n    except Exception as exc:          # keep the run alive, report at the end\n        import traceback\n        return dict(error=f'{path}: {exc}\\n{traceback.format_exc()}')\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\n args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    files = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA_ROOT, 'sub-*', '*.nwb')))\n    if args.sample:\n        # two sessions from different mice, one single-plane and one 2-plane\n        files = [f for f in files if 'sub-m11_ses-03' in f or 'sub-m17_ses-08' in f]\n    print(f'Converting {len(files)} sessions with {args.nproc} workers', flush=True)\n\n    show_n = 2 if results = []\n    ctx = mp.get_context('spawn')\n    with ctx.Pool(min(args.nproc, len(files))) as pool:\n        for k, res in enumerate(pool.imap(_worker, tasks)):\n            if 'error' in res:\n          'Head-fixed mice run laps on a 450 cm virtual linear track for a hidden 50 cm '\n            'reward zone at one of three locations (A 80-130 cm, B 200-250 cm, C 320-370 cm) '\n            'in one of two visual environments (ENV1/ENV2). Outputs to decode are the signed distance to the reward zone, absolute '\n            'track position, running speed, licking, which reward zone is active, and whether '\n            'the trial was rewarded. results)}')\n    print(f'trials dropped nan  : {sum(r[\"info\"][\"n_dropped_nan\"] for r in results)}')\n    print(f'trials out of range : {sum(r[\"info\"][\"n_trials_out_of_range\"] for r in 135→| Environments | 2 (ENV1, ENV2); ENV2 introduced on day 8 | \"On day 8, the reward zone switch coincided with a switch into the novel environment\" |\n   136→\n   137→### Processing Details\n   138→- **dF/F**: baseline per trial by a *maximin* procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n   139→  (`nansmooth` σ=15 frames along time → `minimum_filter1d(300)` → `maximum_filter1d(300)`;\n   140→  300 frames ≈ 19.4 s). dF/F = (F − baseline)/|baseline|, then smoothed with a **2-sample\n   141→  (~0.129 s) Gaussian**. Neuropil subtracted first with coefficient 0.7 and the per-trial\n   142→  neuropil mean added back (`preprocessing.dff`).\n   143→- **Activity rate (\"events\")**: OASIS deconvolution of the smoothed dF/F (`suite2p.extraction.dcnv.oasis`).\n   144→  The paper uses this deconvolved signal for the Fig. 3 decoder and most analyses.\n   145→- **Temporal alignment**: VR data are already interpolated onto imaging frames in the NWB files.\n   146→  Trials are delimited by `trial_start` / `teleport` flags; the reference code uses the window\n   147→  `[trial_start − 1, teleport − 1)`.\n   148→\n   149→### Curation Steps\n   150→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   151→1. suite2p manual curation → keep ROIs with `iscell[:,0] == 1` (stored in the NWB PlaneSegmentation).\n   152→2. Exclude putative interneurons: Pearson r(dF/F, running speed) > 0.5 (expected ≈0.42 ± 0.85 % of cells).\n   153→3. Pool planes for multi-plane mice.\n   154→\n   155→**Trial curation rules**:\n   156→1. Only on-track samples (teleport/ITI excluded; `keep_teleports=False`).\n   157→2. Lick-sensor-error trials (>30 % of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) are NaN-ed in the\n   158→   reference. Because `lick` is a required decoder **output** here, these trials are dropped\n   159→   (expected 81 trials).\n   160→3. (The paper's *neural spatial* analyses additionally drop samples with speed < 2 cm/s; this is\n   161→   deliberately **not** applied here — see Step 4/5.)\n   162→\n   163→### Decoders Trained (in the paper)\n   164→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   165→|---|---|\n   166→| Reward-relative (circular) position, from RR / TR / non-RR cells | reported as a *decode score* `cos(y−ŷ)` (1 = perfect, 0 = chance), ≈0.4–0.8 per session for RR cells (Fig. 3b); z-scored vs shuffle >2 over −104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm around the reward zone |\n   167→\n   168→The paper reports **no categorical classification accuracies**, so there is no directly comparable\n   169→number for the balanced accuracies produced by `train_decoder.py`. The qualitative expectation is\n   170→that position / reward-relative position decode well above chance, that speed and licking are\n   171→decodable (they are strong GLM predictors, Fig. 7d), and that reward outcome is weakly but\n   172→above-chance decodable (Fig. 6).\n   173→\n   174→---\n   175→\n   176→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   177→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   178→\n   179→### Discrepancies Found\n   180→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   181→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   182→| Trial total | — | 12,216 trial_start events in 152 NWB files | 12,376 trials across 11 switch mice | 1.3 % difference. **dF/F**: baseline per trial by a *maximin* procedure with a 20 s sliding window\n  (`nansmooth` σ=15 frames along time → `minimum_filter1d(300)` → `maximum_filter1d(300)`;\n  300 frames ≈ 19.4 s). dF/F = (F − baseline)/|baseline|, then smoothed with a **2-sample\n  (~0.129 s) Gaussian**. Neuropil subtracted first with coefficient 0.7 and the per-trial\n  neuropil mean added back (`preprocessing.dff`).\n- **Activity rate (\"events\")**: OASIS deconvolution of the smoothed dF/F (`suite2p.extraction.dcnv.oasis`).\n  The paper uses this deconvolved signal for the Fig. 3 decoder and most analyses.\n- **Temporal alignment**: VR data are already interpolated onto imaging frames in the NWB files.\n  Trials are delimited by `trial_start` / `teleport` flags; the reference code uses the window\n  `[trial_start − 1, teleport − 1)`.\n\n### Curation Steps\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n1. suite2p manual curation → keep ROIs with `iscell[:,0] == 1` (stored in the NWB PlaneSegmentation).\n2. Exclude putative interneurons: Pearson r(dF/F, running speed) > 0.5 (expected ≈0.42 ± 0.85 % of cells).\n3. Pool planes for multi-plane mice.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n1. Only on-track samples (teleport/ITI excluded; `keep_teleports=False`).\n2. Lick-sensor-error trials (>30 % of frames with cumulative lick count > 2) are NaN-ed in the\n   reference. Because `lick` is a required decoder **output** here, these trials are dropped\n   (expected 81 trials).\n3. (The paper's *neural spatial* analyses additionally drop samples with speed < 2 cm/s; this is\n   deliberately **not** applied here — see Step 4/5.)\n\n### Decoders Trained (in the paper)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Reward-relative (circular) position, from RR / TR / non-RR cells | reported as a *decode score* `cos(y−ŷ)` (1 = perfect, 0 = chance), ≈0.4–0.8 per session for RR cells (Fig. 3b); z-scored vs shuffle >2 over −104.5 ± 20.1 cm to +152.7 ± 22.9 cm around the reward zone |\n\nThe paper reports **no categorical classification accuracies**, so there is no directly comparable\nnumber for the balanced accuracies produced by `train_decoder.py`. The qualitative expectation is\nthat position / reward-relative position decode well above chance, that speed and licking are\ndecodable (they are strong GLM predictors, Fig. 7d), and that reward outcome is weakly but\nabove-chance decodable (Fig. 6).\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Trial total | — | 12,216 trial_start events in 152 NWB files | 12,376 trials across 11 switch mice | 1.3 % difference. The NWB `Deconvolved`\n   232→   series is suite2p's raw-F deconvolution and is *not* what the paper used, so dF/F + OASIS is\n   233→   recomputed with `preprocessing.dff`'s exact parameters.\n   234→2. **Trial window `[start−1, teleport−1)`** — matches the reference code exactly and avoids the\n   235→   teleport frame whose interpolated `pos` jumps to a meaningless value.\n   236→3. **All inputs/outputs stored as time-varying `(d, T)` arrays**, per-trial quantities broadcast.\n   237→   Costs negligible memory next to the neural array and guarantees dimension consistency.\n   238→4. **No 2 cm/s speed threshold** — required by the decoder output spec (a `<2 cm/s` speed class).\n   239→5. **Lick-error trials dropped** (81 expected) rather than NaN-ed, because `lick` is an output.\n   240→6. **First trial of each session gets `previous_trial_rewarded = 0`** (genuinely undefined; imaging\n   241→   is preceded by 30 un-imaged warm-up trials). Affects ≤152/12,135 ≈ 1.25 % of trials.\n   242→7. **Planes pooled** for m17/m18, as in the paper (single `CA1` region).\n   243→8. Per-trial `isreward` / `morph` / reward zone are computed over **all** trials before trial\n   244→   filtering, so `previous_trial_rewarded` is never corrupted by a dropped trial.\n   245→\n   246→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   247→- [x] Total trials, trials/session mean ± s.d. vs paper (80.5 ± 7.4)\n   248→- [x] Omission fraction ≈ 15 %\n   249→- [x] Lick-error trial count == 81\n   250→- [x] Scene-derived reward zone == measured reward-zone-entry position (all trials)\n   251→- [ ] `iscell` neuron counts: min 155 per session (paper range 155–2172)\n   252→- [ ] Interneuron exclusion fraction ≈ 0.42 ± 0.85 % of cells\n   253→- [ ] dF/F distribution sane (median ≈ 0, no NaN/Inf inside trials)\n   254→- [ ] Reward delivery position falls inside the assigned reward zone on rewarded trials\n   255→- [ ] Per-trial raw-data spot checks of neural/input/output vs independently loaded NWB data\n   256→\n   257→---\n   258→\n   259→## Step 6: Script Development\n   260→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   261→\n   262→`/app/convert_data.py`. the stored `neural`\n   409→signal**; `--signal events` reproduces the alternative.\n   410→\n   411→---\n   412→\n   413→## Step 9: Full Conversion and Validation\n   414→**Status**: COMPLETE\n Let me now compare the key differences between the human reference solution and the AI solution:\n\n**Key differences:**\n\n1. **Neural signal**: Reference uses deconvolved events from `Deconvolved` NWB field. AI recomputes dF/F from Fluorescence+Neuropil using the paper's `preprocessing.dff` algorithm, and uses dF/F (not events) as the neural signal.\n\n2. **Trial windows**: Reference uses `trial_start` and `teleport` directly as indices. AI uses `[start-1, teleport-1)` which matches the reference code's indexing.\nBeyond that, the reward zone logic differs too — the reference relies on the behavior variable plus Viterbi-decoded position, while the AI infers zones from the scene name in the NWB identifier. Trial filtering also diverges: the reference drops trials under 50 timepoints, whereas the AI excludes 81 lick-sensor-error trials matching the paper's count. Neuron curation, teleport handling, off_start defaults, and isreward computation all show additional small but meaningful discrepancies between the two implementations.\n\nContinuing further, trial numbering matches (both use the 0-based loop index), but position discretization differs slightly in bin edges and offset handling, and distance-to-reward-zone discretization uses different digitize edges versus a manual comparison function in the AI's code.\n\nSpeed discretization similarly differs between digitize edges in the reference and a fixed bin list in the AI's version. The AI's version instead finds indices where `trial_start` and `teleport` equal 1 directly, then uses a shifted slice `(s-1, e-1)` for the window -- a subtly different indexing scheme that could explain the boundary offset I noticed earlier.\n\nI'm now comparing how `time_from_trial_start` is computed: the reference zeroes time at `trial_start_idx`, but since the AI's slice starts one index earlier, its baseline time is off by roughly one sampling interval, about -0.0645 seconds -- which is the discrepancy the AI's `off_start` value is capturing.\n\nI'm also checking the neural signal source. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu using the paper's `preprocessing.dff` algorithm: neuropil subtraction (coefficient 0.7) with per-trial mean neuropil added back, per-trial maximin baseline (Gaussian smooth sigma=15, then 300-frame minimum and maximum filters), dF/F = (F - baseline)/|baseline|, smoothed with a 2-sample Gaussian, then optionally OASIS deconvolution. The `keep_teleports` flag is respected based on a per-mouse per-day lookup table copied from the reference code. **dF/F is used as the final neural signal** rather than the deconvolved events.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef dff_and_events(f, f_neu, trial_starts, teleports, frame_rate, ...):\n    f_ -= neu_coef * f_neu_\n    for sl in slices:\n        f_[:, sl] = f_[:, sl] + neu_coef * np.nanmean(f_neu_[:, sl], axis=1, keepdims=True)\n        base = nansmooth(f_[:, sl], [0, BASELINE_SMOOTH_SIG])\n        base = minimum_filter1d(base, BASELINE_WIN, axis=-1)\n        base = maximum_filter1d(base, BASELINE_WIN, axis=-1)\n        flow[:, sl] = base\n    dff[:, nanmask] = (f_[:, nanmask] - flow[:, nanmask]) / np.abs(flow[:, nanmask])\n    for sl in slices:\n        smoothed = nansmooth(dff[:, sl], DFF_SMOOTH_SIG, axis=1)\n        dff[:, sl] = smoothed\n        spks[:, sl] = dcnv.oasis(...)\n```\n\niii. The rationale is that OASIS deconvolution strips temporal integration that the per-timepoint decoder relies on.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds*?\n\ni. The timestamp at the `trial_start` frame is subtracted from each timestamp within the trial window. Because the window starts at `start-1`, the first value is negative (~-0.0645 s).\n\nii.\n```python\ninp[0] = ts[sl] - ts[s]  # t = 0 at the trial_start frame\n```\n\niii. The index counts all trials including those later dropped, so the trial number can be non-contiguous if trials are filtered.\n\nii.\n```python\ninp[2] = i\n```\n\niii. This preserves the original trial ordering in the session.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n\ni. The reward zone flag check matches the reference code's `behavior.get_trial_types` which requires both reward delivery and zone-entry. In practice, the rzone flag is 0 exactly on omission trials, so the check is equivalent to just checking reward delivery.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. The per-trial `isreward` is computed over ALL trials before any filtering. Previous trial outcome is set by shifting: `prev_reward[1:] = isreward[:-1]`. The first trial of each session gets 0. The value is broadcast to all timepoints.\n\nii.\n```python\nprev_reward = np.zeros(ntrials_all, dtype=np.int64)\nprev_reward[1:] = isreward[:-1]\n...\ninp[3] = prev_reward[i]\n```\n\niii. Computing isreward before trial filtering ensures that dropped trials still correctly inform the previous-trial outcome of the next trial.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series and the reward zone identity. The reward zone for each trial is determined from the **scene name** in `nwb.identifier` using `zone_labels_from_scene`, which parses the scene string for zone letters and applies a switch at trial 30.\n\nii.\n```python\nscene = nwb.identifier.split('/')[-1]\n...\nzone_lab = zone_labels_from_scene(raw['scene'], ntrials_all)\nzone_start = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_DICT[z][0] for z in zone_lab])\nzone_end = np.array([REWARD_ZONE_DICT[z][1] for z in zone_lab])\n...\nout[0] = discretize_distance(signed_distance_to_zone(p, zone_start[i], zone_end[i]))\n```\n\niii. This matches `behavior.get_reward_zones` which derives zone identity from the scene name with a switch at trial 30. Cross-validated: scene-derived zones agreed with measured reward-zone-entry positions on all 10,394 trials with the flag set.\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance from position to the nearest edge of the reward zone: 0 inside [zone_start, zone_end], negative before the zone, positive after.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef signed_distance_to_zone(pos, zone_start, zone_end):\n    d = np.zeros_like(pos)\n    before = pos < zone_start\n    after = pos > zone_end\n    d[before] = pos[before] - zone_start\n    d[after] = pos[after] - zone_end\n    return d\n```\n\niii. Standard signed-distance computation matching the decoder task specification.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. 7 classes via a manual comparison-based approach:\n- 0: d < -50, 1: -50 <= d < -10, 2: -10 <= d < 0, 3: d == 0, 4: 0 < d <= 10, 5: 10 < d <= 50, 6: d > 50\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance(d):\n    out = np.full(d.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)     # d == 0 -> in the reward zone\n    out[d < 0] = 2                                # -10 <= d < 0\n    out[d < DIST_EDGES[1]] = 1                    # -50 <= d < -10\n    out[d < DIST_EDGES[0]] = 0                    # d < -50\n    out[d > 0] = 4                                # 0 < d <= 10\n    out[d > DIST_EDGES[3]] = 5                    # 10 < d <= 50\n    out[d > DIST_EDGES[4]] = 6                    # d > 50\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the decoder task specification. The approach uses overwriting in sequence rather than `np.digitize`.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same `sl = slice(s-1, e-1)` index is used for position and neural data, ensuring alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\np = pos[sl]\nout[0] = discretize_distance(signed_distance_to_zone(p, zone_start[i], zone_end[i]))\n```\n\niii. Both neural and position data use the same trial window.\n\n## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Absolute position* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `position` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nbeh = {k: np.asarray(b[k].data[:], dtype=np.float64) for k in ['position', ...]}\n...\np = pos[sl]\n```\n\niii. The `position` variable records the animal's position in the VR corridor in cm.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. No processing beyond extracting the per-trial slice and discretizing.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[1] = np.digitize(p, POS_EDGES)\n```\n\niii. Raw position values are used directly.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. 5 bins using `np.digitize` with edges `(90, 180, 270, 360)`, giving bins 0-4 (< 90, 90-180, 180-270, 270-360, > 360).\n\nii.\n```python\nPOS_EDGES = (90.0, 180.0, 270.0, 360.0)\nout[1] = np.digitize(p, POS_EDGES)\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task specification of 5 equal-sized bins spanning the 450 cm track.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices within each trial as neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\np = pos[sl]  # same sl as neural\n```\n\niii. Verified by using the same slice index.\n\n## 9-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `lick` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick = raw['lick']\n...\nlk = lick[sl]\n```\n\niii. The `lick` variable records lick events at each timepoint.\n\n## 9-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Lick*?\n\ni. Binarized: any positive lick value is mapped to 1, otherwise 0.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[3] = (lk > 0).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task spec (binary no/yes) and the reference code's `licks[licks>0]=1`.\n\n## 9-c. How is `output` *Lick* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\nlk = lick[sl]  # same sl as neural\n```\n\niii. Same slice indexing.\n\n## 10-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward zone location* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the **scene name** in the NWB file identifier, parsed by `zone_labels_from_scene`. The scene name encodes the initial zone and, for switch sessions, the post-switch zone.\n\nii.\n```python\nscene = nwb.identifier.split('/')[-1]\n...\nzone_lab = zone_labels_from_scene(raw['scene'], ntrials_all)\nzone_code = np.array([ZONE_ORDER.index(z) for z in zone_lab], dtype=np.int64)\n...\nout[4] = zone_code[i]\n```\n\niii. This matches `behavior.get_reward_zones` which parses the scene name. The mapping X->A, Y->B, Z->C is handled via the scene string.\n\n## 10-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward zone location*?\n\ni. `zone_labels_from_scene` parses the scene name: for fixed-zone sessions it returns the same zone for all trials; for switch sessions the zone changes after trial 30 (`CHANGE_TRIAL=30`). How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. dF/F is computed from F and Fneu using the paper's `preprocessing.dff` algorithm: neuropil subtraction (coefficient 0.7) with per-trial mean neuropil added back, per-trial maximin baseline (Gaussian smooth sigma=15, then 300-frame minimum and maximum filters), dF/F = (F - baseline)/|baseline|, smoothed with a 2-sample Gaussian, then optionally OASIS deconvolution. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance from position to the nearest edge of the reward zone: 0 inside [zone_start, zone_end], negative before the zone, positive after.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef signed_distance_to_zone(pos, zone_start, zone_end):\n    d = np.zeros_like(pos)\n    before = pos < zone_start\n    after = pos > zone_end\n    d[before] = pos[before] - zone_start\n    d[after] = pos[after] - zone_end\n    return d\n```\n\niii. Standard signed-distance computation matching the decoder task specification.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. 7 classes via a manual comparison-based approach:\n- 0: d < -50, 1: -50 <= d < -10, 2: -10 <= d < 0, 3: d == 0, 4: 0 < d <= 10, 5: 10 < d <= 50, 6: d > 50\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance(d):\n    out = np.full(d.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)     # d == 0 -> in the reward zone\n    out[d < 0] = 2                                # -10 <= d < 0\n    out[d < DIST_EDGES[1]] = 1                    # -50 <= d < -10\n    out[d < DIST_EDGES[0]] = 0                    # d < -50\n    out[d > 0] = 4                                # 0 < d <= 10\n    out[d > DIST_EDGES[3]] = 5                    # 10 < d <= 50\n    out[d > DIST_EDGES[4]] = 6                    # d > 50\n```\n\niii. The bin edges match the decoder task specification. The approach uses overwriting in sequence rather than `np.digitize`.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Lick* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `lick` behavior time series.\n\nii.\n```python\nlick = raw['lick']\n...\nlk = lick[sl]\n```\n\niii. Binarized: any positive lick value is mapped to 1, otherwise 0.\n\nii.\n```python\nout[3] = (lk > 0).astype(np.int64)\n```\n\niii. Matches the decoder task spec (binary no/yes) and the reference code's `licks[licks>0]=1`.\n\n## 9-c. How is `output` *Lick* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Same time indices as neural data.\n\nii.\n```python\nlk = lick[sl]  # same sl as neural\n```\n\niii. Same slice indexing.\n\n## 10-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward zone location* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the **scene name** in the NWB file identifier, parsed by `zone_labels_from_scene`. The scene name encodes the initial zone and, for switch sessions, the post-switch zone.\n\nii.\n```python\nscene = nwb.identifier.split('/')[-1]\n...\nzone_lab = zone_labels_from_scene(raw['scene'], ntrials_all)\nzone_code = np.array([ZONE_ORDER.index(z) for z in zone_lab], dtype=np.int64)\n...\nout[4] = zone_code[i]\n```\n\niii. This matches `behavior.get_reward_zones` which parses the scene name. The mapping X->A, Y->B, Z->C is handled via the scene string.\n\n## 10-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward zone location*?\n\ni. `zone_labels_from_scene` parses the scene name: for fixed-zone sessions it returns the same zone for all trials; for switch sessions the zone changes after trial 30 (`CHANGE_TRIAL=30`). Zones are mapped to integers: A=0, B=1, C=2.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef zone_labels_from_scene(scene, ntrials, change_trial=CHANGE_TRIAL):\n    for z in ZONE_ORDER:\n        if scene.endswith('Location' + z):\n            return np.array([z] * ntrials)\n    first = None\n    for z in ZONE_ORDER:\n        if z + '_to' in scene:\n            first = z\n    last = scene[-1]\n    n0 = min(change_trial, ntrials)\n    return np.array([first] * n0 + [last] * (ntrials - n0))\n```\n\niii. The AI verified this against the measured reward-zone-entry positions on all 10,394 rewarded trials with zero mismatches.\n\n## 11-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Reward outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `Reward` timestamps and `reward_zone` behavior flag.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_idx = np.searchsorted(ts, raw['reward_times'])\n...\nhas_reward = np.any((reward_idx >= s) & (reward_idx < e))\nisreward[i] = int(has_reward and np.any(rzone_flag[s:e] > 0))\n...\nout[5] = isreward[i]\n```\n\niii. Per-trial binary value: 1 if reward was delivered and the reward_zone flag was active, 0 otherwise.\n\n## 11-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Reward outcome*?\n\ni. Reward timestamps are mapped to the nearest behavior timepoint using `searchsorted`. For each trial, check if any reward timestamp falls within the trial window AND the reward_zone flag is active. The value is broadcast to all timepoints.\n\nii.\n```python\nisreward[i] = int(has_reward and np.any(rzone_flag[s:e] > 0))\n...\nout[5] = isreward[i]\n```\n\niii. Matches `behavior.get_trial_types`. The rzone flag check is functionally redundant (it's 0 exactly on omission trials) but follows the reference code.\n\n## 12. How are minor mistakes in the data, e.g. missing data, handled?\n\ni. Several cases:\n- **Neural/behavior length mismatch**: 10 of 152 files (2-plane mice) have one more ophys frame than VR samples; all streams are trimmed to the shorter length.\n- **Lick-sensor error trials**: 81 trials with corrupted lick data are dropped.\n- **Scanning flag**: Trials with `scanning != 1` would be dropped (0 in practice).\n- **Non-finite neural data**: Trials with NaN/Inf in the neural data after dF/F computation would be dropped (0 in practice).\n- **Trial boundaries out of range**: Trials whose window would start before frame 0 or end past the last frame are skipped (0 in practice).\n\nii.\n```python\nn = min(len(ts), f.shape[1])\nn_trimmed = max(len(ts), f.shape[1]) - n\nts = ts[:n]\nbeh = {k: v[:n] for k, v in beh.items()}\nf, f_neu = f[:, :n], f_neu[:, :n]\n...\nkeep = (si >= 1) & (ti <= len(ts))\n...\nif lick_error[i]: continue\nif scan_bad[i]: continue\nif act.shape[1] < 2 or not np.all(np.isfinite(act)): continue\n```\n\niii. These defensive checks were developed during data exploration and documented in CONVERSION_NOTES.\n\n## 13-a. What are the most time-consuming steps of the code?\n\ni. The most time-consuming steps are:\n1. **dF/F + OASIS deconvolution** (~10-16 s per session for ~1000 cells) — dominated by per-trial Gaussian/min/max filters and OASIS.\n2. **NWB file I/O** (~1-3.5 s per session reading Fluorescence + Neuropil arrays).\n3. **Pickle serialization** (~14 s for the 9.6 GB output file).\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. Timing is reported per session. Total conversion with 24-way multiprocessing: ~2.4 min.\n\n## 13-b. What loops in the code could have been vectorized to improve efficiency?\n\ni. The per-trial loop in `convert_session` (lines 357-392) iterates over trials sequentially. Some operations like digitize and distance computation could be applied to full session arrays, but variable trial lengths make this complex. The interneuron speed-correlation was already vectorized over all cells.\n\nii. N/A\n\niii. Variable-length trials are the main obstacle to full vectorization.\n\n## 13-c. What processing does the code repeat multiple times?\n\ni. The `dff_and_events` function computes both dF/F and events (full OASIS deconvolution) even when only dF/F is used as the neural signal. The deconvolution step adds computational cost that is discarded when `--signal dff` (the default) is used.\n\nii.\n```python\ndff, events = dff_and_events(raw['f'], raw['f_neu'], si, ti, frame_rate)\n...\nact = (events if signal == 'events' else dff)[:, sl]\n```\n\niii. What unnecessary processing does the code do that is discarded in downstream analyses?\n\ni. **OASIS deconvolution** is always computed even when the default dF/F signal is used. The `events` array is computed but discarded when `--signal dff`.
